Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
- output ports: out[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by `reset`. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, spmv.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019

  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified spmv under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.95433 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 32 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 5.85603e+07 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 4.07248e+08. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in fetcher: no multiply, a register through six LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 5.96 ns in all, 1.60 of it into the third LUT; 7.089 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in a module not established, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with enable; wire 5.51 ns in all, 6.376 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row accumulator; one block per Accumulator instance, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Accumulator instance (32 of them, one block each): clk = clk; reset = rst (the module's own rst input, the global reset); a[17:0] = consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'd0 where mult_out[15:8] is the channel's vec_val carried through fifo_mult and consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg; b[18:0] = {11'b0, mult_out[7:0]} (the channel's mat_val carried through fifo_mult); out[36:0] = new wire acc_out, of which only acc_out[15:0] is used: wr_data_delay = acc_out[15:0] - acc_base, feeding fifo_data_out.din unchanged.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317). Converted as T2: the product is moved past fifo_mult and merged with the row accumulation in Accumulator (spmv.v:3428), so one block per channel does both the 8×8 product and the running sum.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 — all three are the same case and are left for one shared reason. Each is a multiply by a power-of-two constant that exists only to form a shift amount: id >> (counter*8) (spmv.v:2490), data_out >> (counter_delay*16) (spmv.v:163), addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) (spmv.v:172). The "product" is pure wiring (a 5-bit value concatenated with zeros) and the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it, so a block would save no logic and would cost a DSP slot for a renamed wire. Worse, each feeds a combinational barrel shifter whose result is used in the same cycle (data_out_shifted → wr_data → write_mem.din, rd_addr <= at spmv.v:2490), so the block's 2-cycle operand-to-out latency would delay the shift amount by two cycles and change behaviour. No correct, profitable rewrite exists for them.

Why the straightforward T1 on the Channel multiply is impossible, and what replaces it

In the baseline, vec_val/mat_val are the registered RAM outputs of their FIFOs: a read enable sampled at edge E0 makes the popped pair valid in the cycle (E0,E1); at E1 mult <= vec_val*mat_val and mult_wr_en <= 1; at E2 fifo_mult's dpram captures din = mult. The product value is therefore needed at E2, one cycle after its operands become valid. The block needs two (operands registered at E1, out valid only after E2). Delaying the fifo_mult write would move mult_empty, hence the Accumulator's reads, hence row_id_empty/FIFO fullness back into the fetcher — observable on dout/done_reg. So the product cannot stay where it is.

The slack exists one FIFO later: Accumulator has two register stages from mult_out to the output FIFO (wr_data at spmv.v:3423/3428, then wr_data_delay at spmv.v:3414), with only an add between them. That is exactly the block's shape. The rewrite therefore moves the product across fifo_mult:

  1. Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323): replace mult <= vec_val * mat_val; with mult <= {vec_val, mat_val}; — same 16-bit register, same enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, same mult_wr_en. MULT_BITS = 16 = VEC_VAL_BITS + MAT_VAL_BITS, so fifo_mult keeps width 16 and its control (we, re, full, empty, pointers) is bit-for-bit unchanged. The FIFO now carries the operand pair instead of the product. This is invisible outside: mult is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and mult_out only by the two statements in Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428), both of which this plan rewrites.
    Note on stale/never-written FIFO words: entry i of the ref RAM holds v_i*m_i and of the new RAM {v_i,m_i} at the same address written at the same edge, so any read — valid, stale after a reset, or a never-written word (which $init_zero makes 0 in both, and 0*0 = 0) — yields the same value after the block multiplies. No divergence.
  2. Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3439): delete the registers wr_data and wr_data_delay and their adder/mux, and instantiate one block.
verilog
   wire [36:0] acc_out;
   wire consume  = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
   wire new_row  = consume & (row_id != wr_addr);
   reg  new_row_d;
   reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;

   mac_int_18x19 mac (
       .clk(clk), .reset(rst),
       .a(consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'd0),
       .b({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
       .out(acc_out)
   );

   wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay = acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

   always @(posedge clk) begin
       if (rst) begin new_row_d <= 1'b0; acc_base <= 0; end
       else begin
           new_row_d <= new_row;
           if (new_row_d) acc_base <= acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
       end
   end

fifo_data_out .din(wr_data_delay) and .we(wr_en) are untouched (wr_data_delay becomes a wire; remove its reg declaration). The rest of the always block keeps every statement except the three touching wr_data/wr_data_delay: drop wr_data<=0; from the rst branch, drop wr_data_delay <= wr_data;, and in the consume branches keep only wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; / wr_en<=0;. wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, wr_en, both *_rd_en_reg stay exactly as they are.

Why this is the same value. The block holds a global running total of the channel's products; the row sum is a difference against a base snapshot. Write term_k for the product consumed at edge k. The block gives out(k+1) = out(k) + term_k (gated to 0 by consume, which is exactly the branch condition at spmv.v:3420 including first_read at 3416), and the design gives wr_data_delay(k+1) = wr_data(k). The invariant out(k) - acc_base(k) = wr_data(k-1) (mod 2^16) holds by induction: on a same-row consume both sides gain term_k; on a new-row consume wr_data(k) = term_k and new_row_d loads acc_base <= out(k) at edge k+1, giving out(k+1) - acc_base(k+1) = term_k; on every hold (~consume, ~start, the ~first_read branch, the last branch) a is 0 so out holds and acc_base holds. All of start, first_read, row_id_rd_en_reg, mult_rd_en_reg, row_id, wr_addr are valid in the cycle before the edge, so the gate is evaluated at the same time the original branch is. The block wraps at 2^37 and wr_data at 2^16; 2^16 divides 2^37, so truncating the difference to 16 bits is exact.

Reset. reset is tied to rst, the same synchronous active-high reset the module already uses, so blocks in a tile share it. At a rst edge the block clears out, a_reg, b_reg, and acc_base/new_row_d clear to 0 in the same cycle, giving out - acc_base = 0 = wr_data after reset, which re-establishes the invariant after every reset, not only at start-up (the testbench's mid-run 1/2/3-cycle and back-to-back reset pulses included). A term presented into the block in the cycle a reset is sampled is cleared with a_reg — and the design's rst branch (spmv.v:3403) likewise consumes nothing — so no term is gained or lost. The design's wr_data_delay holds its old value through rst and while ~start, where the new expression reads 0; this is unobservable because wr_en is forced to 0 at the reset edge and holds 0 while ~start, and the dpram captures din only when we is high. Power-up: the block's out and the two added soft registers are undefined until the first rst, which the testbench asserts for 4 cycles before comparison begins; nothing relies on their initial value.

No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is equivalent on arbitrary stimulus.

Blocks, tiles, area

32 blocks are added, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and rst, so two fit per tile: 16 DSP tiles, against the baseline's 32 (each holding one mult_9x9_fixed_pt), of 132 available. The 32 soft multipliers that occupied them disappear (Channel now holds a concatenation). That is 16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 minimum-width transistor areas off the baseline's 5.85603e7, ~7%. Soft logic is slightly cheaper too: per channel it loses wr_data (16 FF), wr_data_delay (16 FF) and the 16-bit add + load/accumulate mux, and gains acc_base (16 FF), new_row_d (1 FF), a 16-bit subtractor and the consume gate (which folds into the a input masking as one LUT per bit). With the period unchanged at 6.95433 ns the objective should land near 3.8e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

Timing
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block feeds another; the block's own stage (input register → output register) is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path.
  • Slowest stage is unchanged. The critical path is the fetcher's register → six LUTs → flip-flop (7.089 ns on the seed-1 report, 5.96 ns of it wire); this rewrite does not touch the fetcher, so STAGE_NS stays at the baseline's 6.95433 ns. The rewrite is taken for area, not delay, as the cost guidance allows when the objective is area × delay.
  • Soft logic leaving stages. (a) The combinational 8×8 multiply leaves the stage "operand FIFO dout → mult register" in Channel; that stage becomes a wire into a flip-flop. It is not the slowest stage, so this buys no period. (b) The 16-bit adder and the mult_out/wr_data+mult_out mux leave the stage "fifo_mult dout → wr_data" in Accumulator; that stage becomes RAM clock-to-Q → one LUT (the consume-masked operand) → block input setup (0.019 ns). Not the slowest stage either.
  • Soft logic entering a stage. A 16-bit subtractor is added to the stage "block out (clock-to-Q 0.060) → fifo_data_out dpram write-data setup", which previously was flip-flop → RAM. A 16-bit carry chain plus routing is far below 6.95433 ns — the baseline's second-slowest stage is 6.376 ns with 5.51 ns of that in wire across five LUTs — so this stage does not become the slowest. The acc_out → acc_base path is a clock-to-Q into a flip-flop and is trivially short.
Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Counting from the operands leaving their FIFOs to the value captured by fifo_data_out's RAM:

  • Before: operands → combinational multiply → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → wr_data (4, the add) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO RAM.
  • After: operands → mult, now the concatenated pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → block operand register (4) → block out, product + accumulate (5) → 16-bit subtract → output FIFO RAM.

Five register stages in both cases: the product simply moved three stages later, into the same block that performs the sum, and the block's two registers take over exactly the wr_data and wr_data_delay stages. Every cycle on every output is preserved.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the Channel multiply cannot use the block where it stands because the product must reach fifo_mult's RAM one cycle after its operands leave their FIFOs — one cycle less than the block's operand-to-out latency. If Channel pipelined the write one stage deeper (register mult_wr_en once more and let the block sit between the operand FIFOs and fifo_mult), a block could be used there directly, which would also let the per-channel product and the row accumulation use separate blocks and remove the base-subtract.

1	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
9	// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
10	// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
11	// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
12	// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
13	// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
14	// in output vector.
15	// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
16	// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
17	// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
18	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
19	
20	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
21	// Author: Aatman Borda
22	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
23	
24	//`define SIMULATION

26	`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
27	`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
28	`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
29	`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
30	`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
31	`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
32	`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
33	// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
34	//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
35	`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096

46	`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)

53	`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM (`BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM/`VEC_VAL_BYTES)

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is spmv.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row accumulator; one block per Accumulator instance, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Accumulator instance (32 of them, one block each): clk = clk; reset = rst (the module's own rst input, the global reset); a[17:0] = consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'd0 where mult_out[15:8] is the channel's vec_val carried through fifo_mult and consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg; b[18:0] = {11'b0, mult_out[7:0]} (the channel's mat_val carried through fifo_mult); out[36:0] = new wire acc_out, of which only acc_out[15:0] is used: wr_data_delay = acc_out[15:0] - acc_base, feeding fifo_data_out.din unchanged.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317). Converted as T2: the product is moved past fifo_mult and merged with the row accumulation in Accumulator (spmv.v:3428), so one block per channel does both the 8×8 product and the running sum.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 — all three are the same case and are left for one shared reason. Each is a multiply by a power-of-two constant that exists only to form a shift amount: id >> (counter*8) (spmv.v:2490), data_out >> (counter_delay*16) (spmv.v:163), addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) (spmv.v:172). The "product" is pure wiring (a 5-bit value concatenated with zeros) and the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it, so a block would save no logic and would cost a DSP slot for a renamed wire. Worse, each feeds a combinational barrel shifter whose result is used in the same cycle (data_out_shifted → wr_data → write_mem.din, rd_addr <= at spmv.v:2490), so the block's 2-cycle operand-to-out latency would delay the shift amount by two cycles and change behaviour. No correct, profitable rewrite exists for them.

Why the straightforward T1 on the Channel multiply is impossible, and what replaces it

In the baseline, vec_val/mat_val are the registered RAM outputs of their FIFOs: a read enable sampled at edge E0 makes the popped pair valid in the cycle (E0,E1); at E1 mult <= vec_val*mat_val and mult_wr_en <= 1; at E2 fifo_mult's dpram captures din = mult. The product value is therefore needed at E2, one cycle after its operands become valid. The block needs two (operands registered at E1, out valid only after E2). Delaying the fifo_mult write would move mult_empty, hence the Accumulator's reads, hence row_id_empty/FIFO fullness back into the fetcher — observable on dout/done_reg. So the product cannot stay where it is.

The slack exists one FIFO later: Accumulator has two register stages from mult_out to the output FIFO (wr_data at spmv.v:3423/3428, then wr_data_delay at spmv.v:3414), with only an add between them. That is exactly the block's shape. The rewrite therefore moves the product across fifo_mult:

  1. Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323): replace mult <= vec_val * mat_val; with mult <= {vec_val, mat_val}; — same 16-bit register, same enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, same mult_wr_en. MULT_BITS = 16 = VEC_VAL_BITS + MAT_VAL_BITS, so fifo_mult keeps width 16 and its control (we, re, full, empty, pointers) is bit-for-bit unchanged. The FIFO now carries the operand pair instead of the product. This is invisible outside: mult is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and mult_out only by the two statements in Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428), both of which this plan rewrites.
    Note on stale/never-written FIFO words: entry i of the ref RAM holds v_i*m_i and of the new RAM {v_i,m_i} at the same address written at the same edge, so any read — valid, stale after a reset, or a never-written word (which $init_zero makes 0 in both, and 0*0 = 0) — yields the same value after the block multiplies. No divergence.
  2. Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3439): delete the registers wr_data and wr_data_delay and their adder/mux, and instantiate one block.
verilog
   wire [36:0] acc_out;
   wire consume  = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
   wire new_row  = consume & (row_id != wr_addr);
   reg  new_row_d;
   reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;

   mac_int_18x19 mac (
       .clk(clk), .reset(rst),
       .a(consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'd0),
       .b({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
       .out(acc_out)
   );

   wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay = acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

   always @(posedge clk) begin
       if (rst) begin new_row_d <= 1'b0; acc_base <= 0; end
       else begin
           new_row_d <= new_row;
           if (new_row_d) acc_base <= acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
       end
   end

fifo_data_out .din(wr_data_delay) and .we(wr_en) are untouched (wr_data_delay becomes a wire; remove its reg declaration). The rest of the always block keeps every statement except the three touching wr_data/wr_data_delay: drop wr_data<=0; from the rst branch, drop wr_data_delay <= wr_data;, and in the consume branches keep only wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; / wr_en<=0;. wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, wr_en, both *_rd_en_reg stay exactly as they are.

Why this is the same value. The block holds a global running total of the channel's products; the row sum is a difference against a base snapshot. Write term_k for the product consumed at edge k. The block gives out(k+1) = out(k) + term_k (gated to 0 by consume, which is exactly the branch condition at spmv.v:3420 including first_read at 3416), and the design gives wr_data_delay(k+1) = wr_data(k). The invariant out(k) - acc_base(k) = wr_data(k-1) (mod 2^16) holds by induction: on a same-row consume both sides gain term_k; on a new-row consume wr_data(k) = term_k and new_row_d loads acc_base <= out(k) at edge k+1, giving out(k+1) - acc_base(k+1) = term_k; on every hold (~consume, ~start, the ~first_read branch, the last branch) a is 0 so out holds and acc_base holds. All of start, first_read, row_id_rd_en_reg, mult_rd_en_reg, row_id, wr_addr are valid in the cycle before the edge, so the gate is evaluated at the same time the original branch is. The block wraps at 2^37 and wr_data at 2^16; 2^16 divides 2^37, so truncating the difference to 16 bits is exact.

Reset. reset is tied to rst, the same synchronous active-high reset the module already uses, so blocks in a tile share it. At a rst edge the block clears out, a_reg, b_reg, and acc_base/new_row_d clear to 0 in the same cycle, giving out - acc_base = 0 = wr_data after reset, which re-establishes the invariant after every reset, not only at start-up (the testbench's mid-run 1/2/3-cycle and back-to-back reset pulses included). A term presented into the block in the cycle a reset is sampled is cleared with a_reg — and the design's rst branch (spmv.v:3403) likewise consumes nothing — so no term is gained or lost. The design's wr_data_delay holds its old value through rst and while ~start, where the new expression reads 0; this is unobservable because wr_en is forced to 0 at the reset edge and holds 0 while ~start, and the dpram captures din only when we is high. Power-up: the block's out and the two added soft registers are undefined until the first rst, which the testbench asserts for 4 cycles before comparison begins; nothing relies on their initial value.

No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is equivalent on arbitrary stimulus.

Blocks, tiles, area

32 blocks are added, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and rst, so two fit per tile: 16 DSP tiles, against the baseline's 32 (each holding one mult_9x9_fixed_pt), of 132 available. The 32 soft multipliers that occupied them disappear (Channel now holds a concatenation). That is 16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 minimum-width transistor areas off the baseline's 5.85603e7, ~7%. Soft logic is slightly cheaper too: per channel it loses wr_data (16 FF), wr_data_delay (16 FF) and the 16-bit add + load/accumulate mux, and gains acc_base (16 FF), new_row_d (1 FF), a 16-bit subtractor and the consume gate (which folds into the a input masking as one LUT per bit). With the period unchanged at 6.95433 ns the objective should land near 3.8e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

Timing
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block feeds another; the block's own stage (input register → output register) is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path.
  • Slowest stage is unchanged. The critical path is the fetcher's register → six LUTs → flip-flop (7.089 ns on the seed-1 report, 5.96 ns of it wire); this rewrite does not touch the fetcher, so STAGE_NS stays at the baseline's 6.95433 ns. The rewrite is taken for area, not delay, as the cost guidance allows when the objective is area × delay.
  • Soft logic leaving stages. (a) The combinational 8×8 multiply leaves the stage "operand FIFO dout → mult register" in Channel; that stage becomes a wire into a flip-flop. It is not the slowest stage, so this buys no period. (b) The 16-bit adder and the mult_out/wr_data+mult_out mux leave the stage "fifo_mult dout → wr_data" in Accumulator; that stage becomes RAM clock-to-Q → one LUT (the consume-masked operand) → block input setup (0.019 ns). Not the slowest stage either.
  • Soft logic entering a stage. A 16-bit subtractor is added to the stage "block out (clock-to-Q 0.060) → fifo_data_out dpram write-data setup", which previously was flip-flop → RAM. A 16-bit carry chain plus routing is far below 6.95433 ns — the baseline's second-slowest stage is 6.376 ns with 5.51 ns of that in wire across five LUTs — so this stage does not become the slowest. The acc_out → acc_base path is a clock-to-Q into a flip-flop and is trivially short.
Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Counting from the operands leaving their FIFOs to the value captured by fifo_data_out's RAM:

  • Before: operands → combinational multiply → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → wr_data (4, the add) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO RAM.
  • After: operands → mult, now the concatenated pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → block operand register (4) → block out, product + accumulate (5) → 16-bit subtract → output FIFO RAM.

Five register stages in both cases: the product simply moved three stages later, into the same block that performs the sum, and the block's two registers take over exactly the wr_data and wr_data_delay stages. Every cycle on every output is preserved.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the Channel multiply cannot use the block where it stands because the product must reach fifo_mult's RAM one cycle after its operands leave their FIFOs — one cycle less than the block's operand-to-out latency. If Channel pipelined the write one stage deeper (register mult_wr_en once more and let the block sit between the operand FIFOs and fifo_mult), a block could be used there directly, which would also let the per-channel product and the row accumulation use separate blocks and remove the base-subtract.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll read the design source now.

APPROVE

Checked against spmv.v:

Design facts verified

  • Channel: mult (spmv.v:3285) is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and written only at spmv.v:3317; MULT_BITS = VEC_VAL_BITS + MAT_VAL_BITS = 16 (spmv.v:26-29), so {vec_val, mat_val} is width-exact and the ref's vec_val*mat_val is a 16-bit context product (255*255 fits), i.e. the block's 18×19 product reproduces it bit-for-bit. FIFO control (we, re, full, empty, wp/rp/gb, spmv.v:3639-3673) is untouched, so mult_empty and every downstream handshake are unchanged.
  • mult_out is read only at spmv.v:3423 and spmv.v:3428 (via Channel_Accumulator, spmv.v:3222/3235/3248); it leaves no other module. Visibility holds.
  • wr_data read only at spmv.v:3414/3428; wr_data_delay read only by fifo_data_out.din (spmv.v:3376). Both are safely replaced.
  • consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg is exactly the branch condition spmv.v:3409/3416/3420, and new_row matches spmv.v:3421.
  • $init_zero in sim_reference/tb_equiv_spmv.v:52 makes unwritten dpram words 0 in both, so the stale/never-written-word argument holds (0 vs 0·0).

Equivalence
The invariant out_k − acc_base_k ≡ wr_data_{k−1} (mod 2^16) checks out by induction over all four branches, including consecutive new-row consumes (acc_base reloads correctly from new_row_d), and 2^16 | 2^37 makes the truncated difference exact.

Reset / power-up
reset = rst is synchronous active-high as the block requires. At a rst edge the block clears out/a_reg/b_reg — dropping the in-flight term exactly as spmv.v:3403-3404 drops it — and acc_base/new_row_d clear with it. The one window where the new wr_data_delay wire differs from the original register (the reset cycle and the following ~start cycles, where the original holds its old value) is unobservable: wr_en <= 0 at the reset edge (spmv.v:3405), start can only fall via rst (spmv.v:143-154, start is never cleared otherwise), and the Accumulator updates nothing while ~start, so wr_en stays 0 and fifo_data_out's dpram never captures. On resume, the ~first_read branch realigns both sides at 0. This covers the testbench's 1/2/3-cycle and back-to-back mid-run pulses (tb_equiv_spmv.v:144-152). No ASSUMES: is needed — the rewrite is unconditional.

Left regions
spmv.v:163, spmv.v:172, spmv.v:2490 are all x >> (counter*CONST) shift-amount products feeding same-cycle combinational shifts (data_out_shifted → wr_data → write_mem.din at spmv.v:175/212; rd_addr <= at spmv.v:2490); the shared reason given is accurate.

Required items all present: four candidates accounted for (1 converted, 3 grouped with reason); 32 blocks added, 16 DSP tiles used (one rst for all, two per tile, 16 ≤ 132); longest series of blocks = one, 2.219 ns against the 6.95433 ns slowest stage; soft logic leaving each stage named with its slowest-stage status, plus the subtractor entering the output stage; register stages before and after given as 5 and 5.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

prompt
Task

Edit spmv.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh spmv.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row accumulator; one block per Accumulator instance, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Accumulator instance (32 of them, one block each): clk = clk; reset = rst (the module's own rst input, the global reset); a[17:0] = consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'd0 where mult_out[15:8] is the channel's vec_val carried through fifo_mult and consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg; b[18:0] = {11'b0, mult_out[7:0]} (the channel's mat_val carried through fifo_mult); out[36:0] = new wire acc_out, of which only acc_out[15:0] is used: wr_data_delay = acc_out[15:0] - acc_base, feeding fifo_data_out.din unchanged.

PLAN:

Candidate regions

Converted: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — mult <= vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317). Converted as T2: the product is moved past fifo_mult and merged with the row accumulation in Accumulator (spmv.v:3428), so one block per channel does both the 8×8 product and the running sum.

Left: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 — all three are the same case and are left for one shared reason. Each is a multiply by a power-of-two constant that exists only to form a shift amount: id >> (counter*8) (spmv.v:2490), data_out >> (counter_delay*16) (spmv.v:163), addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) (spmv.v:172). The "product" is pure wiring (a 5-bit value concatenated with zeros) and the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it, so a block would save no logic and would cost a DSP slot for a renamed wire. Worse, each feeds a combinational barrel shifter whose result is used in the same cycle (data_out_shifted → wr_data → write_mem.din, rd_addr <= at spmv.v:2490), so the block's 2-cycle operand-to-out latency would delay the shift amount by two cycles and change behaviour. No correct, profitable rewrite exists for them.

Why the straightforward T1 on the Channel multiply is impossible, and what replaces it

In the baseline, vec_val/mat_val are the registered RAM outputs of their FIFOs: a read enable sampled at edge E0 makes the popped pair valid in the cycle (E0,E1); at E1 mult <= vec_val*mat_val and mult_wr_en <= 1; at E2 fifo_mult's dpram captures din = mult. The product value is therefore needed at E2, one cycle after its operands become valid. The block needs two (operands registered at E1, out valid only after E2). Delaying the fifo_mult write would move mult_empty, hence the Accumulator's reads, hence row_id_empty/FIFO fullness back into the fetcher — observable on dout/done_reg. So the product cannot stay where it is.

The slack exists one FIFO later: Accumulator has two register stages from mult_out to the output FIFO (wr_data at spmv.v:3423/3428, then wr_data_delay at spmv.v:3414), with only an add between them. That is exactly the block's shape. The rewrite therefore moves the product across fifo_mult:

  1. Channel (spmv.v:3312-3323): replace mult <= vec_val * mat_val; with mult <= {vec_val, mat_val}; — same 16-bit register, same enable vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg, same mult_wr_en. MULT_BITS = 16 = VEC_VAL_BITS + MAT_VAL_BITS, so fifo_mult keeps width 16 and its control (we, re, full, empty, pointers) is bit-for-bit unchanged. The FIFO now carries the operand pair instead of the product. This is invisible outside: mult is read only by fifo_mult.din (spmv.v:3299) and mult_out only by the two statements in Accumulator (spmv.v:3423, 3428), both of which this plan rewrites.
    Note on stale/never-written FIFO words: entry i of the ref RAM holds v_i*m_i and of the new RAM {v_i,m_i} at the same address written at the same edge, so any read — valid, stale after a reset, or a never-written word (which $init_zero makes 0 in both, and 0*0 = 0) — yields the same value after the block multiplies. No divergence.
  2. Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3439): delete the registers wr_data and wr_data_delay and their adder/mux, and instantiate one block.
verilog
   wire [36:0] acc_out;
   wire consume  = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
   wire new_row  = consume & (row_id != wr_addr);
   reg  new_row_d;
   reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;

   mac_int_18x19 mac (
       .clk(clk), .reset(rst),
       .a(consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'd0),
       .b({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
       .out(acc_out)
   );

   wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay = acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;

   always @(posedge clk) begin
       if (rst) begin new_row_d <= 1'b0; acc_base <= 0; end
       else begin
           new_row_d <= new_row;
           if (new_row_d) acc_base <= acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
       end
   end

fifo_data_out .din(wr_data_delay) and .we(wr_en) are untouched (wr_data_delay becomes a wire; remove its reg declaration). The rest of the always block keeps every statement except the three touching wr_data/wr_data_delay: drop wr_data<=0; from the rst branch, drop wr_data_delay <= wr_data;, and in the consume branches keep only wr_en<=1; wr_addr<=row_id; / wr_en<=0;. wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, wr_en, both *_rd_en_reg stay exactly as they are.

Why this is the same value. The block holds a global running total of the channel's products; the row sum is a difference against a base snapshot. Write term_k for the product consumed at edge k. The block gives out(k+1) = out(k) + term_k (gated to 0 by consume, which is exactly the branch condition at spmv.v:3420 including first_read at 3416), and the design gives wr_data_delay(k+1) = wr_data(k). The invariant out(k) - acc_base(k) = wr_data(k-1) (mod 2^16) holds by induction: on a same-row consume both sides gain term_k; on a new-row consume wr_data(k) = term_k and new_row_d loads acc_base <= out(k) at edge k+1, giving out(k+1) - acc_base(k+1) = term_k; on every hold (~consume, ~start, the ~first_read branch, the last branch) a is 0 so out holds and acc_base holds. All of start, first_read, row_id_rd_en_reg, mult_rd_en_reg, row_id, wr_addr are valid in the cycle before the edge, so the gate is evaluated at the same time the original branch is. The block wraps at 2^37 and wr_data at 2^16; 2^16 divides 2^37, so truncating the difference to 16 bits is exact.

Reset. reset is tied to rst, the same synchronous active-high reset the module already uses, so blocks in a tile share it. At a rst edge the block clears out, a_reg, b_reg, and acc_base/new_row_d clear to 0 in the same cycle, giving out - acc_base = 0 = wr_data after reset, which re-establishes the invariant after every reset, not only at start-up (the testbench's mid-run 1/2/3-cycle and back-to-back reset pulses included). A term presented into the block in the cycle a reset is sampled is cleared with a_reg — and the design's rst branch (spmv.v:3403) likewise consumes nothing — so no term is gained or lost. The design's wr_data_delay holds its old value through rst and while ~start, where the new expression reads 0; this is unobservable because wr_en is forced to 0 at the reset edge and holds 0 while ~start, and the dpram captures din only when we is high. Power-up: the block's out and the two added soft registers are undefined until the first rst, which the testbench asserts for 4 cycles before comparison begins; nothing relies on their initial value.

No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is equivalent on arbitrary stimulus.

Blocks, tiles, area

32 blocks are added, one per Accumulator (one per channel). All share clk and rst, so two fit per tile: 16 DSP tiles, against the baseline's 32 (each holding one mult_9x9_fixed_pt), of 132 available. The 32 soft multipliers that occupied them disappear (Channel now holds a concatenation). That is 16 × 253779 ≈ 4.06e6 minimum-width transistor areas off the baseline's 5.85603e7, ~7%. Soft logic is slightly cheaper too: per channel it loses wr_data (16 FF), wr_data_delay (16 FF) and the 16-bit add + load/accumulate mux, and gains acc_base (16 FF), new_row_d (1 FF), a 16-bit subtractor and the consume gate (which folds into the a input masking as one LUT per bit). With the period unchanged at 6.95433 ns the objective should land near 3.8e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

Timing
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block feeds another; the block's own stage (input register → output register) is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path.
  • Slowest stage is unchanged. The critical path is the fetcher's register → six LUTs → flip-flop (7.089 ns on the seed-1 report, 5.96 ns of it wire); this rewrite does not touch the fetcher, so STAGE_NS stays at the baseline's 6.95433 ns. The rewrite is taken for area, not delay, as the cost guidance allows when the objective is area × delay.
  • Soft logic leaving stages. (a) The combinational 8×8 multiply leaves the stage "operand FIFO dout → mult register" in Channel; that stage becomes a wire into a flip-flop. It is not the slowest stage, so this buys no period. (b) The 16-bit adder and the mult_out/wr_data+mult_out mux leave the stage "fifo_mult dout → wr_data" in Accumulator; that stage becomes RAM clock-to-Q → one LUT (the consume-masked operand) → block input setup (0.019 ns). Not the slowest stage either.
  • Soft logic entering a stage. A 16-bit subtractor is added to the stage "block out (clock-to-Q 0.060) → fifo_data_out dpram write-data setup", which previously was flip-flop → RAM. A 16-bit carry chain plus routing is far below 6.95433 ns — the baseline's second-slowest stage is 6.376 ns with 5.51 ns of that in wire across five LUTs — so this stage does not become the slowest. The acc_out → acc_base path is a clock-to-Q into a flip-flop and is trivially short.
Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Counting from the operands leaving their FIFOs to the value captured by fifo_data_out's RAM:

  • Before: operands → combinational multiply → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → wr_data (4, the add) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO RAM.
  • After: operands → mult, now the concatenated pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM (2) → fifo_mult dout register (3) → block operand register (4) → block out, product + accumulate (5) → 16-bit subtract → output FIFO RAM.

Five register stages in both cases: the product simply moved three stages later, into the same block that performs the sum, and the block's two registers take over exactly the wr_data and wr_data_delay stages. Every cycle on every output is preserved.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

response

I'll start by reading the design and the relevant regions.

Now the Channel edit:

Let me verify the cause of the X-divergence with a controlled diagnostic (local sim copies only).

What I changed

check.sh spmv.v passes; the census shows 32 mac_int_18x19 instances (one per Accumulator), no $mul anywhere, and the 32 new $sub cells — exactly the blocks the plan calls for.

Region Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 — converted (32 blocks)

Channel (spmv.v:3317). mult <= vec_val * mat_val; → mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};. Same 16-bit register, same enable, same mult_wr_en; fifo_mult keeps width MULT_BITS (= 8+8) and all of its control untouched. mult feeds only fifo_mult.din, and mult_out is read only at the two statements this plan rewrites, so the FIFO now carries the operand pair invisibly.

Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3473). Deleted the wr_data register, the wr_data_delay register and the adder/load mux (wr_data<=0 in the rst branch, wr_data_delay <= wr_data, and the two wr_data <= … assignments). Added, per the plan's mapping:

  • mac_int_18x19 mac with .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'd0), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}), .out(acc_out[36:0]) — every input port connected by name;
  • consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg, new_row = consume & (row_id != wr_addr), the soft registers new_row_d and acc_base (both cleared by rst), and wire [15:0] wr_data_delay = acc_out[15:0] - acc_base; feeding fifo_data_out.din unchanged.

wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last, done, wr_en and both *_rd_en_reg are byte-for-byte as before.

Left alone, as the plan directs

bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 — untouched.

Verification, and one failure I must report

I built a local equivalence run (iverilog, the reference tb_equiv_spmv.v/drive_spmv.vh/mac_int_18x19.v, a reconstructed pristine original as spmv_ref, and a VPI $init_zero). Result on the unmodified design: 541 mismatch cycles out of 17311 — all of them dout reading xxxx in my version where the reference has a defined value, zero value mismatches, done_reg clean, and all of load, run1, the mid-run reset pulse and run2 (~14 100 cycles) clean. The first divergence is 107 cycles into the random phase.

Cause: bvb.vector_ram (spmv.v:1985-1995) is a 129-word spram addressed by 8 bits (BVB_AWIDTH = COL_ID_BITS = 8), read at rd_addr taken from a column id (spmv.v:2490). The testbench's random phase drives full 8-bit col_id_din, so addresses 129..255 read out of range and return X — in the reference as well (it reports "x on reference: dout 6864"). The reference's wr_data <= mult_out reloads from scratch at each new row, so that X is flushed one row later; the block's running total is sticky — once an X term is added to out, out and therefore acc_out - acc_base stay X until the next rst.

I confirmed this is the sole cause: re-running with the spram array widened to (1<<AWIDTH) words in both copies (a change to the local sim copies only, never to /work/spmv.v) gives 0 mismatches and 0 X on 17311/17311 cycles, including the mid-run reset pulse and the 1-in-512 random resets. So the arithmetic, the acc_base/new_row_d invariant and the reset derivation are exact.

The step that cannot be made to hold is the plan's claim in Accumulator rewrite, "Why this is the same value" / "No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is equivalent on arbitrary stimulus." It is equivalent in two-state arithmetic but not under X propagation, and no variant of the planned structure fixes it: the block's only clear is reset, which also wipes the operand register, so clearing between rows destroys the row's first term, and the original pipeline (mult_out valid at cycle k-1, din needed at cycle k+1) leaves zero slack to re-present it. Masking the X away is no better — the reference does put X on dout for those 6864 cycles, so the rewrite would have to reproduce the X footprint exactly, not suppress it. I did not improvise a different rewrite; the plan is in place as written.

ASSUMES: every column id reaching bvb indexes a word that exists in vector_ram, i.e. rd_addr <= 128 (spmv.v:1966, 1978, 2490 drive an 8-bit rd_addr into vector_ram, declared with NUM_WORDS(NUM_ADDR) = 129 at spmv.v:1985 and AWIDTH(BVB_AWIDTH) = 8 at spmv.v:1984, with NUM_ADDR and BVB_AWIDTH at spmv.v:56-57). Under that condition the rewrite is cycle-exact; the testbench's random phase violates it, which is where the 541 X-only mismatch cycles come from.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

module Channel(
	input clk, 
	input rst,
	input start,
	input fetcher_done,

	input [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mat_val,
	input mat_val_empty,
	output mat_val_rd_en,

	input [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vec_val,
	input vec_val_empty,
	output vec_val_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	output mult_empty,
	input mult_rd_en,

	output done 
	);

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH = 8;
	// parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = `LOG2(FIFO_DEPTH);
	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(8);

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult;
	reg mult_wr_en;
	wire mult_full;
	
	reg vec_val_rd_en_reg;
	reg mat_val_rd_en_reg;

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.dw(`MULT_BITS),
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS)
		) fifo_mult (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(mult),
		.we(mult_wr_en),
		.re(mult_rd_en),
		.dout(mult_out),
		.full(mult_full),
		.empty(mult_empty)
	);

	assign done = mat_val_empty & fetcher_done & start;

	assign vec_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);
	assign mat_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
		mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;

		if(vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg) begin
			mult <= vec_val * mat_val;
			mult_wr_en <= 1;
		end
		else begin
			mult_wr_en <= 0;
		end
	end
endmodule

module Accumulator(
	input clk, 
	input rst, 
	input start, 
	input mult_done,

	input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id,
	input row_id_empty,
	output row_id_rd_en,

	input [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	input mult_empty,
	output mult_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out,
	output data_out_empty,
	output [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out,
	input out_rd_en,

	output reg done
	);
	
	reg first_read;

	reg row_id_rd_en_reg;
	reg mult_rd_en_reg;

	wire data_out_full;
	wire addr_out_full_nc;

	wire addr_out_empty_nc;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr;
	reg wr_en;

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay;
	reg [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr_delay;

	reg last;

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(`FIFO_DEPTH);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`MULT_BITS)
	) fifo_data_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_data_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(data_out),
		.full(data_out_full),
		.empty(data_out_empty)
	);

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS),
		.dw(`ROW_ID_BITS)
	) fifo_addr_out (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(wr_addr_delay),
		.we(wr_en),
		.re(out_rd_en),
		.dout(addr_out),
		.full(addr_out_full_nc),
		.empty(addr_out_empty_nc)
	);

	assign row_id_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);
	assign mult_rd_en = start & (~row_id_empty) & (~mult_empty) & (~data_out_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			wr_data<=0;
			wr_en<=0;
			first_read <= 0;
			done <= 0;
		end 
		else if(start) begin
			last <= mult_done & row_id_empty;
			row_id_rd_en_reg <= row_id_rd_en;
			mult_rd_en_reg <= mult_rd_en;
			wr_addr_delay <= wr_addr;
			wr_data_delay <= wr_data;

			if(~first_read) begin
				wr_addr <= row_id;
				first_read <= 1;
			end 		
			else if(row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg) begin
				if(row_id!=wr_addr) begin
					wr_en <= 1;
					wr_data <= mult_out;
					wr_addr <= row_id;
				end
				else begin
					wr_en <= 0;
					wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out;
				end
			end
			else if (last) begin
				wr_en <= 1;
				done <= 1;
			end
			else begin
				wr_en <= 0;
			end
		end
	end
endmodule

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule
// target block: mac_int_18x19

	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] vec_val_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] vec_val_rd_en;

	// 1 if at least 1 of the fetcher FIFOs is empty
	reg all_empty;

	// Signal to start the engine
	reg start;

	// Output data
	wire [(`MULT_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] data_out;
	// output fifo empty signals
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] data_out_empty;
	// Address to store output data
	wire [(`ROW_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] addr_out;
	// output fifo read enable signal
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en;
	// reg [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en_reg;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en_shifted;
	// Memory to write the output data

	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out_shifted;
	wire [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out_shifted;
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data;
	wire [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr;
	reg wr_en;

	// Signals to indicate that the computation is complete
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] done;
	reg done_all, last;

	wire fetcher_done;

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if (rst) begin
			all_empty <= 1;
			start <= 0;
		end
		else begin
			all_empty <= (|fetcher_empty) & (~start);
			if (!all_empty) begin
				start <= 1;
			end
		end
	end

	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter;
	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter_delay;
	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter_store;

	assign out_rd_en = (({`NUM_CHANNEL{1'b0}})|(1<<counter)) & ~data_out_empty;
	assign out_rd_en_shifted = out_rd_en >> counter;

	assign data_out_shifted = data_out >> (counter_delay*`MULT_BITS);
`ifdef QUARTUS
	genvar loop_idx;
	generate
		for (loop_idx = 0; loop_idx < `ROW_ID_BITS; loop_idx = loop_idx + 1) begin: gen_addr_out
			assign addr_out_shifted[loop_idx] = ^(addr_out[(loop_idx+1)*`NUM_CHANNEL-1:loop_idx*`NUM_CHANNEL]);
		end
	endgenerate
`else
	assign addr_out_shifted = addr_out >> (counter_delay*`ROW_ID_BITS);
`endif

	assign wr_data = data_out_shifted;
	assign wr_addr = addr_out_shifted;

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			counter <= 0;
			counter_delay <= 0;
			last <= 0;
		end
		else if(start) begin
			done_all <= &done;
			counter <= counter + 1;
			counter_delay <= counter;
			if(out_rd_en_shifted[0]) begin
				wr_en <= 1;
			end
			else begin
				wr_en <= 0;
			end
			if(done_all & !last) begin
				counter_store <= counter_delay;
				last <= 1;
			end
			else if (last) begin
				done_reg <= (counter_store==counter_delay);
			end
		end
	end

	spram #(
		.AWIDTH(`ROW_ID_BITS),
		.NUM_WORDS(`NUM_VEC_VALS),
		.DWIDTH(`MULT_BITS)
	) write_mem (
		.clk(clk),
		.address(wr_addr),
		.wren(wr_en),
		.din(wr_data),

3364a3368,3401
> 	// ---- hard-block row accumulator -------------------------------------
> 	// mac_int_18x19 holds a running total of every product this channel consumes
> 	// (a is masked to 0 on a cycle that is not consumed, so out only moves on a
> 	// consume). The row sum is the difference between that total and a base
> 	// snapshot taken when a new row starts. The block's two registers take over
> 	// exactly the wr_data and wr_data_delay stages of the original.
> 	wire [36:0] acc_out;
> 	wire consume = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
> 	wire new_row = consume & (row_id != wr_addr);
> 	reg  new_row_d;
> 	reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] acc_base;
> 
> 	mac_int_18x19 mac (
> 		.clk(clk),
> 		.reset(rst),
> 		.a(consume ? {10'b0, mult_out[`MULT_BITS-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'd0),
> 		.b({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
> 		.out(acc_out)
> 	);
> 
> 	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data_delay = acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - acc_base;
> 
> 	always@(posedge clk) begin
> 		if(rst) begin
> 			new_row_d <= 1'b0;
> 			acc_base <= 0;
> 		end
> 		else begin
> 			new_row_d <= new_row;
> 			if(new_row_d) acc_base <= acc_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
> 		end
> 	end
> 	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------
>

{
 "file_path": "/tmp/eqtools/init_zero.c",
 "content": "/* VPI implementation of $init_zero(model_str, scope, scope, ...):\n   zero every reg and memory word in each named scope, recursively, skipping\n   the hard-block model's own registers (its contract says they are X until reset). */\n#include <vpi_user.h>\n#include <string.h>\n#include <stdio.h>\n\nstatic long long nzero = 0;\n\nstatic void put_zero(vpiHandle h)\n{\n    s_vpi_value v;\n    v.format = vpiIntVal;\n    v.value.integer = 0;\n    vpi_put_value(h, &v, 0, vpiNoDelay);\n    nzero++;\n}\n\nstatic void zero_scope(vpiHandle scope)\n{\n    const char *def = vpi_get_str(vpiDefName, scope);\n    vpiHandle it, h;\n\n    if (def && strstr(def, \"mac_int_18x19\")) return;  /* leave the block model X */\n\n    it = vpi_iterate(vpiReg, scope);\n    if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) put_zero(h);\n\n    it = vpi_iterate(vpiMemory, scope);\n    if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) {\n        vpiHandle it2 = vpi_iterate(vpiMemoryWord, h), w;\n        if (it2) while ((w = vpi_scan(it2))) put_zero(w);\n    }\n\n    it = vpi_iterate(vpiModule, scope);\n    if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) zero_scope(h);\n}\n\nstatic PLI_INT32 init_zero_calltf(PLI_BYTE8 *ud)\n{\n    vpiHandle sys = vpi_handle(vpiSysTfCall, 0);\n    vpiHandle it = vpi_iterate(vpiArgument, sys), a;\n    (void)ud;\n    nzero = 0;\n    if (it) while ((a = vpi_scan(it))) {\n        int t = vpi_get(vpiType, a);\n        if (t == vpiModule) zero_scope(a);\n    }\n    vpi_printf(\"init_zero: %lld locations set to 0\\n\", nzero);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nstatic void register_tf(void)\n{\n    s_vpi_systf_data d;\n    memset(&d, 0, sizeof d);\n    d.type = vpiSysTask;\n    d.tfname = \"$init_zero\";\n    d.calltf = init_zero_calltf;\n    d.compiletf = 0;\n    vpi_register_systf(&d);\n}\n\nvoid (*vlog_startup_routines[])(void) = { register_tf, 0 };\n"
}
